國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
企業活動維持擴張，消費信心與房市疲弱，通膨及關稅風險仍存 
經濟 
⚫ 10 月美國綜合 PMI 產出指數初值由 58.4 降至 57.6，企業活動仍強勁擴
張，內需及出口帶動訂單與就業成長；惟油價、關稅及供應鏈瓶頸推升
成本，售價漲幅創 2022 年 8 月以來新高，通膨壓力持續升溫，後續須
關注聯準會政策動向。 
⚫ 9 月美國住宅市場續顯疲弱，建築許可及新屋開工分別年減 3.0%及
7.4%，反映住宅投資動能放緩。儘管多戶住宅許可與完工數回升，但整
體營建活動仍偏弱，後續仍須關注房貸利率、住房負擔能力及營建成本
對市場復甦的影響。 
⚫ 密西根大學公布之 10 月美國消費者信心指數降至 48.0，月減 0.2%、年
減 10.4%，顯示民眾對目前經濟環境仍缺乏信心，雖然對未來景氣的看
法略有改善，但高物價與借貸成本仍讓消費者感到壓力。此外，未來一
年及長期通膨預期分別升至 4.7%及 3.5%，反映民眾仍擔心物價持續上
漲。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 10 月 23 日川普宣布調整加拿大商品 50%關稅清單，10 月 30 日生效，
豁免部分紙製品、機械及包裝材料，並新增地毯、油漆等課稅項目。外
媒認為此舉與期中選舉有關，有助降低部分企業成本，但美加貿易摩擦
仍未化解。 
短評 
⚫ 10 月美國綜合產出指數雖略為下滑，但仍維持高檔，顯示內需與就業持
續支撐企業活動擴張。然而，消費者信心仍處低檔，反映高物價與借貸
成本持續壓抑購買意願，儘管民眾對未來景氣的看法略有改善，但新屋
開工及建築許可同步下滑，顯示消費與住宅投資前景仍面臨挑戰。另一
方面，美國調整部分加拿大商品關稅，雖有助減輕部分企業進口成本，
但雙邊貿易摩擦尚未化解，政策不確定性仍存。

法國動盪短期內難降溫，料將使央行保持審慎觀望態度 
經濟 ⚫ 歐元區 10 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)下降 1.3 點至 51.9，服務業、製 造業 PMI 分別降 1.3、0.5 點至 51.7、52.9。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 歐洲議會代表團於 10 月 27 日至 29 日訪問中國，料將討論稀土及綠能
技術的依賴、貿易失衡，以及歐洲企業在中國面臨的不平等待遇問題。 
⚫ 法國抗爭活動規模逐漸擴大，各大工會與抗議團體已宣布在 10 月 31 日
再度發起大規模遊行，並預告於 11 月 5 日舉行全國性大罷工。 
短評 
⚫ 法國政府既無法強力通過重大的財政改革，也難以快速推出安撫反對派
的妥協法案或者公共支出。部分較為悲觀的機構認為此波動盪可能會延
續至 2027 年 4 月的法國總統大選。 
⚫ 市場預期歐洲央行在 10 月 28 日的政策會議將採取觀望立場。 
日 
本 
貿易逆差及通膨壓力延續，內需仍具韌性 
經濟 
⚫ 9 月出口年增 16.7%。 
⚫ 9 月全國消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 2.1%。 
⚫ 10 月綜合 PMI 持平於 52.3。 
短評 
⚫ 出口維持強勁、服務需求亦持續擴張，惟中東局勢推升能源進口成本，
使貿易逆差擴大，並加重國內通膨壓力。隨成本上漲逐步由商品擴散至
服務價格，通膨黏著性可能進一步提高，為日本央行貨幣政策正常化提
供支撐；另一方面，原物料供應受限及投入成本居高不下，已開始影響
製造業動能與企業信心，後續仍須關注高成本環境對經濟成長的制約。 
中 
國 
第三季經濟成長率回升，但前景仍較為不利 
經濟

⚫ 1-9 月累計零售額年增率持平為 1.1%。 
⚫ 1-9 月固定資產投資年減幅維持於 7.2% 
⚫ 1-9 月房地產開發投資額年減幅由 19.9%擴大至 20.6%。

生產年增率持平 1-8 月水準為 5.3%。

事件

品、家具及機械等產品，不再僅限電動車。 
⚫ 中國銀行成功發行市場首筆掛鉤 DR001 基準利率的「三農」專項金融

外銷訂單創新高，產業景氣冷熱不均 
經濟 ⚫ 9 月外銷訂單達 1,142.1 億美元，年增 62.4%。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.10.27 
一、 美國—企業活動維持擴張，消費信心與房市疲弱，通膨及關稅風險仍存 
標普全球(S&P Global)公布，2026 年 10 月美國綜合 PMI 產出指數(Composite 
PMI Output Index)初值由上月的 58.4 降至 57.6，其中服務業採購經理人指數
(Services PMI)由 58.8 降至 57.9，製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI)則由
55.9 微升至 56.0，創 2022 年 5 月以來新高，企業活動雖略有放緩，但仍維持強
勁擴張。需求方面，內需穩健及出口改善帶動整體新訂單增速創逾 4 年半新高，
商品出口訂單亦為 2025 年 6 月以來首次增加；企業持續擴增人力，整體就業增
速為 2022 年 6 月以來次高。另一方面，未完成訂單增加、供應商交貨延遲加劇，
加上油價上漲及關稅推升能源、運輸與原物料成本，使企業投入成本維持高檔，
帶動售價漲幅升至 2022 年 8 月以來最高。儘管企業對未來一年營運前景的樂觀
程度略降，但仍處於今年相對高檔。整體而言，美國經濟在內外需及就業支撐下
維持擴張，惟供應鏈瓶頸與成本上升加劇通膨壓力，後續仍須關注物價走勢及聯
準會貨幣政策動向。 
密西根大學公布，2026 年 10 月美國消費者信心指數終值降至 48.0，略低於
上月的 48.1，月減 0.2%、年減 10.4%，並較今年 2 月下降約 15%，顯示消費者信
心持續處於低檔。分項來看，當前經濟狀況指數由 50.9 降至 46.7，月減 8.3%、
年減 20.3%；消費者預期指數則由 46.3 回升至 48.8，月增 5.4%、年減 3.0%，反
映民眾對未來景氣的看法略有改善，但整體信心仍偏弱。調查指出，高利率及購
買負擔壓力使耐久財購買條件下降 18%，拖累當前經濟狀況指數；未來一年企業
景氣預期雖改善約 14%，但仍低於去年同期 16%。通膨預期方面，未來一年通膨
預期由 4.6%升至 4.7%，長期通膨預期亦由 3.4%升至 3.5%，雙雙連續第二個月攀
升，創今年 5 月以來最高水準。密西根大學指出，2025 年關稅政策衝擊及 2026
年伊朗衝突引發的能源價格上漲，使民眾通膨預期再度升溫，且長期預期已明顯
高於 2019 年及 2024 年水準。整體而言，10 月美國消費者信心大致持平，但高物
價與借貸成本仍壓抑購買意願，加上短期及長期通膨預期持續上升，可能增加物
價回穩的難度，後續仍須關注能源價格、家庭消費及通膨預期變化對聯準會貨幣
政策的影響。

2026 年 9 月美國住宅市場建築許可年化總數降至 140.0 萬戶，月減 0.2%、年
減 3.0%，其中單戶住宅建築許可降至 84.5 萬戶，月減 4.0%、年減 4.8%，惟 5 戶
以上多戶住宅建築許可增至 50.3 萬戶，月增 5.9%、年增 1.0%，顯示不同住宅類
型開發規劃表現分歧。新屋開工年化數降至 122.1 萬戶，月減 5.2%、年減 7.4%，
其中單戶住宅開工 85.9 萬戶，較去年同期仍增 2.6%，但多戶住宅開工降至 34.9
萬戶，年減 25.9%，拖累整體營建動能。施工方面，在建住宅維持 128.0 萬戶，與
上月持平但較去年減少 3.3%；完工年化數則回升至 119.2 萬戶，月增 2.2%，主要
受多戶住宅完工月增 14.9%帶動，惟整體仍較去年同期減少 14.0%。整體而言，
美國住宅市場呈現建築許可及新屋開工下滑、在建規模持平及完工略有回升的態
勢，反映住宅營建活動仍偏弱，後續仍須關注房貸利率走勢、住房負擔能力及營
建成本變化對住宅市場復甦的影響。 
美國總統川普 10 月 23 日依據《1930 年關稅法》第 338 條簽署公告，調整部
分加拿大商品加徵 50%關稅的適用範圍，並自 10 月 30 日起生效。此次調整取消
膠合板、部分紙製品、機械與冷凍設備、包裝用木箱及紙箱、新鮮與乾燥海藻等
商品的額外關稅，同時新增閥門、地毯、油漆、洗髮精、可可粉、木製棺材、空
調設備及獨木舟等課稅項目，呈現局部鬆綁與新增課稅並行的政策方向。白宮表
示，此舉旨在減輕加拿大貿易措施對美國企業造成的負擔，並維護國內產業利益。
由於緬因州農業及製造業高度依賴加拿大進口物資，共和黨參議員柯林斯(Susan 
Collins)積極向白宮爭取關稅豁免，外媒亦將此次調整與 11 月 3 日期中選舉連結。
整體而言，新措施有助降低部分美國企業的進口成本，但美加貿易摩擦尚未完全
化解，後續仍須關注雙邊談判及關稅政策對供應鏈的影響。 
二、歐元區—法國動盪短期內難降溫，料將使央行保持審慎觀望態度 
 歐元區 10 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)下降 1.3 點至 51.9，抵銷上月的強勁
增幅，但為連續五個月高於 50 的榮枯線，主要係受到法國大規模抗議活動的影
響，使法國綜合 PMI 下降 2.7 點至 48.4；此外，德國綜合 PMI 下降 1 點至 52.8，
亦反映近期通膨壓力增加，使民眾傾向保守消費。若以產業區分，歐元區服務業、
製造業 PMI 分別降 1.3、0.5 點至 51.7、52.9；惟德國製造業 PMI 上升至相當強勁
的 54.2，反映德國政府擴張軍備投資以及基礎建設之需求，持續推動製造業生產
動能。

6

法國抗爭活動規模逐漸擴大，各大工會與抗議團體已宣布在 10 月 31 日再度
發起大規模遊行，並預告於 11 月 5 日舉行全國性大罷工，抗議主要訴求包括改
善公共服務品質及凍結物價上漲。然而，在財政壓力巨大且國會缺乏穩定執政多
數的情況下，法國政府既無法強力通過重大的財政改革，也難以快速推出安撫反
對派的妥協法案或者公共支出。故部分較為悲觀的機構認為此波動盪可能會延續
至 2027 年 4 月的法國總統大選。受法國局勢影響，近期歐元區各國殖利率普遍
呈現上升趨勢，並對景氣產生暫時性的抑制效果。在此背景下，市場預期歐洲央
行在 10 月 28 日的政策會議將採取觀望立場，後續關注歐洲央行是否將透過政策
訊號或流動性工具，為平息法國主權債務利率而採取行動。 
三、日本—貿易逆差及通膨壓力延續，內需仍具韌性 
 9 月出口雖維持強勁，受半導體製造設備及汽車出口增加所帶動，出口額年增
16.7%；惟進口增幅更為顯著，9 月進口額年增 18.9%，其中原油及液化天然氣進
口額分別大增 51.1%及 39.2%，主要反映中東局勢推升能源價格，以及日本尋求
替代能源供應來源的影響。由於進口額高於出口額，9 月貿易逆差達 5,365 億日
圓，為連續第 5 個月呈現逆差；累計 2026 上半財年（4 至 9 月）貿易逆差亦較上
年同期擴大 98.8%至 2.82 兆日圓。整體而言，出口並未明顯轉弱，近期貿易逆差
擴大主要反映能源進口成本攀升，使進口增幅持續高於出口。 
 9 月全國消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 2.1%，即使政府透過能源及教育補貼等措
施抑制物價，通膨仍高於 2%，顯示實際通膨動能更為強勁。從物價結構來看，石
油、原物料及工資成本上升已逐步傳導至終端價格，漲價範圍由家具、醫療用品
及休閒用品等商品，進一步擴散至房屋維護、公共運輸、住宿及外食等服務項目，
顯示企業成本轉嫁範圍逐步擴大，通膨壓力亦由商品向服務領域擴散。隨通膨持
續高於 2%且漲價範圍擴大，亦為日本央行進一步推動貨幣政策正常化提供支撐。 
 10 月綜合 PMI 持平於 52.3，顯示經濟仍維持穩定擴張，其中製造業 PMI 下
降 0.8 點至 53.3，產出指數亦降至 3 月以來最低，主要反映新訂單及出口訂單成
長放緩，加上稀土金屬等原料供應受限，對生產形成一定壓力，惟生產與訂單仍
處擴張區間，尚未顯示製造業景氣明顯轉弱；服務業 PMI 回升 0.7 點至 52.0，新
業務及出口訂單同步改善，顯示服務需求仍具韌性，為支撐景氣的重要動能。另
一方面，能源、薪資及原物料成本居高不下，使投入價格壓力持續偏高，並逐漸

### Page 8

用相關內容。

影響企業對未來景氣的信心。 
四、中國—第三季經濟成長率回升，但前景仍較為不利 
2026 年第三季 GDP 年增率初估值為 4.5%，較第二季的 4.3%上揚 0.2 個百分
點；累計 1-9 月 GDP 年增率則為 4.6%。第三季的二、三級產業年增率分別較第
二季上揚 0.4、0.1 個百分點至 3.4%，其中製造業、批發零售業年增率分別達 5.9%、
2.3%為主要動能，抵銷建築業年減 4.4%等拖累因素影響。整體而言，第三季 GDP
年增率上揚有部分受到中秋國慶連假落在 9 月末段(去年同期則於 10 月 1 日開
始)，致基期效果發揮部分影響，相關消費動能仍可能會在 10 月出現暫時性的回
落。 
在需求指標方面，(1)1-9 月累計零售額年增率為 1.1%，持平於 1-8 月的累計
年增率。其中 9 月零售額年增率增加 0.7 個百分點至 1.1%，部分受到新手機上市
帶動買氣，9 月通訊器材消費年增率高達 31.1%，加上中秋國慶長假帶動食品消
費年增率增加 1 個百分點至 2.1%所致；(2)1-9 月固定資產投資年減幅維持於 7.2%，
反映供應鏈移轉、政府主導去化過剩產能等因素導致民間投資需求急遽萎縮；萎
縮產業包含化工、醫藥、專用設備、汽車製造業、金屬製品業等；(3)1-9 月房地
產開發投資額年減幅由 19.9%擴大至 20.6%、施工面積年減幅為 12.8%，新建商
品房銷售面積年減幅擴大為 13.2%，房市投資、買氣皆持續低迷。 
受到出口維持高度成長帶動，9 月工業生產年增率較 8 月增加 0.3 個百分點
至 5.5%(累計 1-9 月工業生產年增率持平 1-8 月水準為 5.3%)。依產業別來看，9
月主要受到煤礦開採、化工、化纖等產業擴張影響，主因中秋與國慶長假前發電
業充實庫存水位，促使煤炭企業加快復產，加上油價上漲帶動相關中下游供應鏈
增產；惟高科技產品包含 IC、機器人、新能源車等年增率皆有放慢，反映去年中
秋國慶連假皆落在 10 月，使得去年 9 月工作日較多，致基期相對墊高的影響。 
展望未來，儘管政府 10 月初宣布釋放 5,500 億元人民幣的地方政府債務結存
限額，但額度下達到資金撥付往往需要數月時間，預期對於第四季的經濟影響有
限，使今年全年經濟成長率預估將落在 4.5%附近。此外，受歐盟右派勢力崛起及
保護主義影響，歐盟對中國貿易政策可能持續趨嚴，後續仍須關注雙方談判及溝
通結果，未來若外需動能減弱，預期中國政府將持續透過發展特定高科技產業及
扶植內需來支持經濟成長動能，以減輕房市不振及貿易環境惡化的衝擊。

8

五、印度及東南亞—新、馬出口保持強勁增速，菲律賓央行大舉升息 
(1)印度: 10 月 HSBC 綜合採購經理人指數（PMI）初值上升 0.8 點至 56.7，其中，
製造業 PMI 略下滑 0.2 點至 54.9、服務業 PMI 則上揚 1.0 點至 56.2，皆保持穩健
擴張態勢。由於美國、英國、中東及孟加拉等地的訂單增加，帶動新訂單及出口
訂單加速成長，製造商採購庫存增速創八個月來最快、成品庫存增速亦創歷史新
高，然而聘僱指數降至今年以來最低、未完成訂單下降，反映現階段人力充足。
另一方面，投入成本漲幅連續第七個月下降並降至近一年低點，產品售價漲幅亦
明顯放緩，通膨壓力緩解，有助提升企業獲利能力及消費者購買力，並且給予印
度央行（RBI）貨幣政策空間。 
(2)新加坡：9 月非石油國內出口（NODX）年增 68.4%，增速史上最快，連續第
23 個月正成長，其中，人工智慧需求強勁，帶動電子產品出口躍增 107.6%；非電
子產品出口亦大幅成長 54.2%，主要由船舶和遊艇組件所帶動。按出口目的地劃
分，對香港出口激增 121.1%、對台灣、韓國出口增幅達 96%，對美出口成長 73.2%，
但對印度出口下降 5.6%。展望未來，新加坡金融管理局在 10 月的宏觀經濟評估
報告中表示，直至 2027 年人工智慧 (AI) 驅動的成長將保持相對穩定，支持未
來幾季經濟成長表現。由於基數較高以及人工智慧相關投資預計放緩，全球利率
上升可能會對新加坡主要貿易夥伴的消費和非人工智慧投資造成壓力，可能會影
響外部需求，薪資成長放緩、招聘謹慎以及運輸成本上升可能會在 2026 年下半
年對可自由支配支出和零售活動構成壓力，預計 2027 年 GDP 成長將放緩。 
(3)印尼：印尼央行（BI）10 月會議連續第四次會議維持基準利率(7 天期逆回購利
率,BI-Rate)於 5.75%不變，貸款便利利率（LFR）維持在 6.50%不變，但將隔夜存
款便利利率（DFR）調高 25 個基點至 5%，旨在提高印尼盾資產的回報率，藉此
吸引並留住外資，同時宣布分兩階段調降存款準備率，11 月 1 日起下調 1 個百分
點至 8%，12 月 1 日起進一步降至 7%，以提供銀行體系流動性。整體而言，印尼
央行爲捍衛印尼盾，在 5 至 6 月間共升息 100 個基點，同時調降外資避險成本，
並採取「資金停留愈久、優惠愈高」的機制吸引外資流入。惟近期國際指數編纂
公司 MSCI 擬將印尼股市由”新興市場”降級為”獨立市場”，引發資金外流疑慮，
央行選擇利率按兵不動，搭配降準提供銀行體系流動性，以兼顧印尼盾穩定與國
內經濟需求，未來 MSCI 檢討進展(最快 2027 年 4 月公布)及印尼盾走勢，將持續
牽動央行後續政策。

9

(4)菲律賓：菲律賓央行（BSP）10 月 22 日宣布大幅調升基準利率(逆回購利率)2
碼至 5.50%，為 2023 年以來最大幅度升息。央行指出， 9 月通膨率已升至 7.2%，
遠高於 2%-4%的目標區間；同時核心通膨持續攀升，顯示價格上漲已由食品與能
源擴散至整體經濟。BSP 亦擔憂中東情勢推升國際油價、運輸費率調漲，以及聖
嬰現象衝擊農業生產，進一步推高物價。此外，披索貶至歷史低點附近，增加進
口通膨風險，因此採取較激進的緊縮措施以穩定市場預期，貨幣政策重點已由支
持經濟成長轉向優先遏制通膨風險。 
(5)馬來西亞：9 月出口額年增 54.5%至 MYR2,151 億元，其中電子&電氣產品出
口大增 69.2%，持續反映 AI 帶動的強勁需求，礦產品出口亦大幅成長 56.3%，抵
銷農產品出口續跌 5.7%的影響，對台灣、美國出口大增 175.2%、89.9%、對新加
坡出口增幅亦達 50.4%。9 月進口額成長 52.7%至 MYR1,814 億元，受惠出口引
申需求，中間產品、資本貨物進口分別成長 69.6%、20.9%，消費品進口轉為正成
長 18.2%，自中國和新加坡進口分別成長 67.6%、90.7%。進出口相抵，9 月貿易
順差達 MYR337 億元、年增 64.9%。 
六、台灣—外銷訂單創新高，產業景氣冷熱不均 
9 月外銷訂單達 1,142.1 億美元，再創單月新高，年增 62.4%，主要受惠於人
工智慧、高效能運算與雲端服務等應用蓬勃發展，以及消費性電子新品陸續推出，
帶動相關供應鏈維持高檔；加上國際原物料價格上漲，推升整體接單表現。惟各
產業接單動能仍呈分化，科技產品需求強勁，傳統產業景氣則相對疲弱。經濟部
指出，雖有年底節慶補貨潮挹注，但傳統產業部分產能過剩，終端需求仍未見明
顯回升。

CPO: New growth opportunities from vertical integration — Hon Hai sees
significant long-term opportunities in both ASIC and vertical integration including
optical interconnect technologies. In optical networking, management believes the
transition from electrical to optical interconnects is structurally inevitable as AI
models expand and memory-bandwidth requirements increase. Hon Hai’s CPO

optical switching already starts mass volume production for its major customer,
and its micron-level precision manufacturing expertise in optical alignment,
connectors, and electrical-to-optical conversion could create additional value
through component integration and higher system-level content. Its extensive
engineering resources, ODM/JDM development capabilities, and vertically
integrated manufacturing platform should strengthen its competitive positioning.

Management expressed strong confidence in optical growth opportunities but did
not provide specific 2027 revenue guidance.

Robust financials and earnings growth, maintain Buy — Thanks to strong
demand from major CSP clients and its continuous automation and vertical
integration development, Hon Hai’s capex increased 43% YTD. Nevertheless, its
EBITDA increased 50.3% YoY to NT$369.2bn. To improve capital efficiency, Hon

Hai plans to expand consignment arrangements, optimize inventory and
receivables management, and carefully evaluate investment payback periods.
Management reiterated its long-term operating margin floor of above 3%, with
further potential upside from scale economies, automation, product mix
improvements, and greater vertical integration.
Given Hon Hai’s solid result and better operational leverage, we raise our
2026/2027/2028 EPS by 4%/5%/5%. Considering its solid execution, stable

dividend yield and attractive valuation, we reiterate our Buy rating with TP NT$400
unchanged. Our TP implies 15x of our 2027 EPS projection.

Hon Hai Precision (2317.TW)

9
Hon Hai Precision
Company description
Established in 1974, Hon Hai has become the largest EMS player in the world
in terms of sales. Through its vertical integration model or "eCMMS" (e-
enabled Components, Modules, Moves and Services), it provides
components, modules and system assembly products to many global OEMs
in the PC, communication and consumer sectors.
Investment strategy
We rate Hon Hai shares as Buy as we believe the company’s margins will

continue to improve, backed by a renewed focus on profitability, operational
efficiency and new businesses development. We think there’s more upside to
go from current level, as the underlying drivers are not just about product
cycle, but also about fundamental transformation and new business
opportunities.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$400 is based on 15x average 2026E-27E EPS, which is
at the higher end of the historical trading range. We believe this multiple is

justified by the company’s enhanced sales growth and profit margin outlook,
as well as new endeavors into AI server, EV, digital health and robotics. Hon
Hai has been able to generate positive earnings streams, and thus we believe
a P/E valuation methodology is appropriate.
Risks
Downside risks that could impede the stock from reaching our target price
include: 1) slower-than-expected iPhone demand; 2) intensified peer
competition; 3) weaker-than-expected execution on new business progress;

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds

### Page 1

Summary: Merida hosted its 3Q26 investor call on 17 November after market hours, 
following release of its 3Q26 results on 11 November (see our memo). Merida’s gross 
margin came in at 15.2% (vs. 19.8% in 2Q26; 13.7% in 3Q25), lower than our estimate 
(16.2%) and the consensus (17.3%). Factoring in the equity investment loss of 
TWD150m+, its 3Q26 EPS was TWD1.06, lower than our forecast (TWD1.68) and the 
consensus estimate (TWD1.65).

We have a Buy (1) rating with a 12-month TP of TWD96, based on a target PER of 17x 
applied on our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. For more information on this company, please 
refer to our latest flash note, 2Q26 review: mainly dragged by Specialized, on 14 August.

What’s the impact 
• 3Q26 results recap. Merida’s 3Q26 revenue of TWD6,098m (-12.1% QoQ, -15.6% 
YoY) missed our and the Bloomberg consensus estimates by 13.6% and 11.1%, 
respectively, which we attributed to the discount when destocking the older models. Its 
gross margin came in at 15.2% (vs. 19.8% in 2Q26; 13.7% in 3Q25), lower than our 
estimate (16.2%) and the consensus (17.3%), due to less OBM sales, the price discount 
for further inventory digestion and the depreciation of the Euro. Factoring in the equity 
investment loss of TWD150m+, its net profit of TWD176m (-44.8% QoQ, -71.9% YoY) 
missed our and the consensus estimates by 65.0% and 65.6%. Its 3Q26 EPS was 
TWD1.06, lower than our forecast (TWD1.68) and the consensus estimate (TWD1.65).  
• 2026 outlook update. Merida revised down its 2026 full-year shipments to 960k units 
(Taiwan plant 280k, down from previous 340k; China plant 680k, down from previous 
700k), as it continues to manage its existing model shipments and expects new models 
in 2027 to bring stronger demand. The company now sees recovery in Europe and 
China, with inventory digestion coming to an end: 9M26 sales in Europe grew 14% YoY, 
driven by strong demand for road bikes and gravel bikes; 9M 26 sales in China grew by 
1% while shipments grew by 7% (vs. revenue guidance of declining 15-20% at the 
beginning of 2026), driven by low- and mid-end models. 
• SBC expected to be profitable in 4Q26. Due to SBC’s conservative tax assumption 
(over 100% effective tax rate) in 1Q-3Q26, if and when the tax rate realised at year-end 
is determined to be less than currently allocated for, we expect there will be a huge profit 
recognised in 4Q26 to offset the estimated after-tax loss recognised in 1Q-3Q26. Merida 
and SBC will review the tax assumption policy after full-year results are out, as the 
policy has caused a great deal of investor confusion. Including USD15m of tariff refund 
in 3Q26, SBC’s pre-tax profit grew by 42% YoY for 9M26. Merida expects there is 
another USD5m of tariff refund to collect in 4Q26.

What we recommend 
We have a Buy (1) rating with a 12-month TP of TWD96, based on a target PER of 17x 
applied on our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. The stock is currently trading at PERs of 
13.2x/10.6x, based on our 2026/27E EPS, vs. its past-5-year one-year-forward PER 
trading range of 7-33x. Key downside risks: slower-than-expected e-bike penetration in the 
EU and the US, worse-than-expected demand in an inflationary environment and inventory 
digestion.

In the interests of timeliness, this document has not been edited.

Feedback: Read-across from
Three-Circle's comments for
Yageo
Positive pricing signals for Yageo: Chaozhou Three-Circle (300408.SZ/6951.HK,
not covered) spoke at our Asia Pacific Summit. It said that its 3Q26 MLCC ASPs rose
20–30% q/q on catch-up increases in mid/low-capacitance products. We see this as

supportive of firmer standard MLCC pricing at Yageo.
Tightness extends into 1H27: Three-Circle said its utilization rate was at 100% and
it expects high-capacitance shortages through 1H27. Management said Murata/
SEMCO’s capacity shifts were happening and this would continue to tighten
standard MLCC supply, supporting our view on Yageo (rated Overweight) and its
utilization and pricing.
Technology limits competitive overlap: Three-Circle said its  AI mix was single-digit,

with small, high-capacitance MLCCs still a capability gap. This suggests limited
immediate competition for the more demanding products within Yageo’s high-
capacitance portfolio.
Three-Circle said its >40% CY26 capacity growth outlook implies >70B MLCC
units/month by year-end, versus ~50B at the start of the year. This compares to
our estimate on Yageo at ~80B/month, although we don't know the exact
specifications used by either company, so that could limit direct capacity

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

Yageo Corp. (2327.TW)

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

1 
4540 全球傳動(TBI MOTION)

報告日期：2026/11/19 研 究 員：洪 國 峰 (Titan Horng)  
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 3Q26 財報受惠產品組合優化，呆帳回沖，業外匯兌利益挹注，
單季 EPS 0.79 元，優於先前預期 0.44 元。 
 蘇州新廠將於 1Q~2Q27 正式投產，將有助舒緩台灣樹林廠產
能吃緊情況。 
 桃園大溪廠己於 4Q26 正式處分，預計 1Q27 交割完成，處分
利益 1.1 億元，貢獻全球傳動 EPS 0.95 元。 
一. 公告 3Q26 稅後 EPS 0.79 元 
在客戶訂單回溫，全球傳動 3Q26 營收金額 9.46 億元，
YoY+40.27%，QoQ+13.96%，營業利益 0.81 億元，較 3Q25 由
虧轉盈，QoQ+63.74%，稅後盈餘 0.92 億 元，較 3Q25 由虧轉盈，
QoQ+80.7%，稅後 EPS 0.79 元。1Q~3Q26 稅後 EPS 1.04 元，
優於去年同期-1.08 元。 
二. 預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 0.56 元 
全球 10/2026 營收金額 2.88 億元，YoY+11.5%，MoM-11.14%。
全球傳動 4Q26 將致力優化產品組合為目標，預估全球傳動 4Q26
營收金額 9.36 億元，YoY+21.5%，QoQ-1.05%，預估營業利益
0.59 億元，較 4Q25 由虧轉盈，QoQ-27.6%，預估稅後盈餘 0.64
億元，YoY+38.2%，QoQ-30.1%，預估稅後 EPS 0.56 元。 
三. 預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 1.60 元 
全球傳動 2026 年傳動元件產品如行星滾柱螺桿與微型螺桿持續
切入機器人，半導體與自動化設備領域，產能利用率回溫，帶動
由虧轉盈。預估全球傳動 2026 年營收金額 35 億元，YoY+36.8%，
預估稅後盈餘 1.84 億元，由虧轉盈，預估稅後 EPS 1.60 元。 
四. 預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 3.30 元 
全球傳動 2027 年在中國大陸持續大力發展機器人領域，將有助全
球行星滾柱螺桿與微型螺桿產品切入，另外大溪廠處分利益 1Q27
認列，有助全球傳動 2027 年營運表現。預估全球傳動 2027 年營
收金額 40 億元，YoY+14.3% ，預估稅後盈餘 3.80 億元，
YoY+105.97%，預估稅後 EPS 3.30 元。 
五. 投資建議買進，目標價 66 元 
觀察機器人執行機構件包括旋轉執行器，線性執行器，末端執行
器，全球傳動己小量成功打入線性執行器與靈巧手廠商供應鏈，
將有助評價提升，故投資建議買進，目標價 66 元。(28X 2027 年
扣除大溪廠土地後稅後 EPS=20X 2027 年稅後 EPS )

2

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
全球傳動科技(TBI MOTION)成立於 2010 年，資 本 額 11.52 億元，
並於 2018 年掛牌上市，全球傳動為台灣傳動元件專業製造廠，TBI 
MOTION 掌握關鍵核心技術，專注創新研發與設計，擁有多項產
品設計專利並且通過 ISO9001 認證，經銷營業據點分佈全球，廣
獲各國客戶的肯定。TBI MOTION 產品線齊全、MIT 台灣製造生
產，主要產品：滾珠螺桿、線性滑軌、滾珠花鍵、旋轉式滾珠螺
桿/花鍵、單軸機器人、直線軸承、聯軸器、螺桿支撐座..等。 產
品的應用範圍非常廣泛，實際應用於自動化產業、半導設備、產
業機械、工具機產業、綠能產業與機器人相關產業。全球傳動營
收地區方面，中國大陸 74~82%、台灣地區 7~10%、亞洲地區
6~10%、歐洲地區 3~5%、美洲地區 1%。

圖二、全球傳動產品

資料來源：公司&宏遠投顧彙整

預估 2027 年滾珠螺桿營收金額 21.00 億元，YoY+12.9%

滾珠螺桿主要功用在於藉由鋼珠於螺桿與螺帽之間進行運動，由
於滾珠螺桿在頻繁返復運轉時，鋼珠間會產生摩擦熱度導致出現
氧化或脫碳現象而縮短使用壽命，全球傳動滾珠螺桿以轉造級螺
桿為主，所提供標準規格為 6mm~80mm，亦可提供研磨級螺桿規
格為 4mm~160mm，螺帽部分則以循環方式區分內循環、外循環、
端蓋式循環。滾珠螺桿 2025 年營收金額 14.73 億元，
YoY+0.00%，在中國大陸製造業景氣低迷，導致客戶訂單量維持
在低檔水準。

滾珠螺桿 1Q~3Q26 營收金額 13.59 億元，YoY+33.27%。預 期 2026
年，全球傳動己切入人形機器人的關鍵零組件，包括線型執行器
與靈巧手。觀察一台人型機器人需要用到 14 個線型執行器，一個
線型執行器需要一台行星滾柱螺桿；靈巧手方面，一台人型機器
人需要兩隻靈巧手，每隻靈巧手需要 15 支微型滾珠螺桿。上述都
己分別打入台灣與中國大陸機器人相關供應鏈，預期 2026 年開始
小量出貨。另外半導體則切入後段封裝設備廠，預期機器人及半
導體可望為營運成長動能。宏遠預估 2026 年滾珠螺桿相關產品營
收金額 18.60 億元，YoY+2633%。

預期 2027 年，在滾珠螺桿在自動化設備與機器人市場規模持續擴
大帶動下，另外微小型研磨螺桿己打入 CPO 設備模組供應鏈，將
有助滾珠螺桿出貨持續成長，宏遠預估 2027 年滾珠螺桿相關產品
營收金額 21.00 億元，YoY+12.9%。

預估 2027 年線性滑軌營收金額 16.35 億元，YoY+14.3%

線性滑軌屬滾動導引裝置，主要藉由鋼珠於滑塊與滑軌之間作無
限滾動循環，以維持負載平台順沿滑軌進行高精度線性運動，其
中技術層次要求須將線性滑軌摩擦係數降至傳統滑動導引的
1/50，以及滑塊與滑軌間末制單元設計須能同時承受各方向負
荷。全球傳動標準型線性滑軌尺吋涵蓋 15mm~65mm，另可提供
7mm~15mm 之微小型滑軌。線性滑軌 2025 年營收金額 9.41 億
元，YoY+35.2%。

線型滑軌 1Q~3Q26 營收金額 10.51 億元，YoY+58.3%。預 期 2026
年，全球傳動在中國自動化設備、電動車及鋰電池等新能源應用
帶動下，將推升線性滑軌出貨量，宏遠預估 2026 年線型滑軌營收
金額 14.30 億元，YoY+52.0%。

預期 2027 年，在中國自動化產業需求維持高檔水準，加上其他產
業需求持續增加，線性滑軌訂單能見度己看到 1Q~2Q27，宏遠預
估 2027 年線型滑軌營收金額 16.350 億元，YoY+14.3%。

預估 2027 年滾珠花鍵與其他營收金額 2.65 億元，YoY+26.2%

滾珠花鍵特色相較於滾珠花鍵可大負荷容量、旋轉方向零間隙、
高度靈敏性、高剛性與裝配簡單等。其他產品主要以單軸機器人
為主，單軸機器人採用線軌與螺桿的優點，將螺帽與滑塊設計成
一體式機構，並搭配高剛性的 U 型軌最佳化斷面，可達到最佳的
空間節省及大幅度減少組裝的時間，確保其高剛性、高精度要求。
滾珠花鍵與其他產品 2025 年營收金額 1.46 億元，YoY+16.1%。

在自動化智慧工廠趨勢明確，全球將持續開發新產品，加上產能
擴充帶動，將有助滾珠花鍵與單軸機器人恢復成長動能，預估滾
珠花鍵與其他 2026 年營收金額 2.1 億元，YoY+44.03%。

Document 245: [034020] 두산에너빌리티(034020) 펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한 일시적 주가 하락두산에너빌리티(034020) 펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한_07bab1f0

Source PDF: all report pdf/[034020] 두산에너빌리티(034020) 펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한 일시적 주가 하락두산에너빌리티(034020) 펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한_07bab1f0.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

(034020) 
두산에너빌리티 
2026.10.30

에너지/인프라/배터리 황성현, Ph.D. 
6878 / tjdgus2009@eugenefn.com

펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한 일시적 주가 하락 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 80,000 원(하향) 
 현재주가 56,700 원(10/29)

 [투자의견] 당사는 두산에너빌리티에 대해 목표주가를 80,000원(2027년 
BPS의 6.2배)으로 27% 하향하며, 투자의견 BUY를 유지한다. 실적은 견조
한 회복을 보이고 있으나, 최근 GE Vernova(GEV) 등 글로벌 AI 및 에너지 
밸류체인 Peer 주가 조정을 반영해 멀티플을 하향 조정했다. 체코 시공 계
약 등 일부 노이즈에 의해 주가가 급락한 만큼 매수 기회라고 판단한다.  
 [3Q26 Review] 매출액 4.4조원(-5.8%qoq, +14.7%yoy), 영업이익 
2,612억원(-16.9%qoq, +90.6%yoy)을 기록해 당사 추정에 부합하는 무
난한 실적을 기록했다. 에너빌리티 부문은 매출액 1.8조원, 영업이익 1,211
억원(영업이익률 6.6%, +2.3%p qoq)을 기록하며 원자력·가스터빈 중심의 
고수익 사업 믹스 개선으로 분기 수익성이 재차 개선됐다. 당기순이익은 
149억원(-93.4%qoq)에 그쳤으나, 원달러 환율 급락으로 발생한 환손실 
반영 때문으로 이익 체력과는 무관하다고 판단한다. 3Q26 미국 빅테크 데
이터센터향 가스터빈 첫 출하를 달성했으며, 수주잔고는 에너빌리티 부문 
기준 26.6조원(+70.9%yoy)으로 사상 최대치를 경신했다. 
 [4Q26 Preview] 매출액 4.8조원, 영업이익 2,640억원으로 유사한 실적 
흐름을 전망한다. 4Q 가스터빈 인도와 체코 두코바니 원전 진행률 반영으로 
에너빌리티 부문 외형 성장이 기대된다. 3Q26 별도 기준 영업이익률이 
8.5%까지 개선된 만큼 에너빌리티 부문의 분기 실적 개선 추세는 유효하다
고 판단한다. 연간 수주 목표 13.3조원 달성을 위한 잔여 수주(3.3조원 +α)
가 예정되어 있으며, 대형원전을 제외하더라도 가이던스 달성은 무난할 것
이라 판단한다. 세부적으로는 SMR 1조원(계약 조건 합의 완료 단계로 연내 
체결 가능), 신재생 7,000억원(해상풍력 기자재·EPC 2건 협의 완료 단계), 
해외 복합 EPC 5,000억원(국내 1건, 해외 2건)이 기대된다. 
 [가스터빈] 3Q26 누적 가스터빈 13기·스팀터빈 11기·LTSA 6건을 달성하
며 연간 10기 이상 수주·수행 역량이 안정적으로 유지되고 있다. 2030년 
슬롯 예약 물량의 본계약 전환은 2027년, 2031년 슬롯은 2028년으로 예
상되며, 수주잔고 증가가 기대된다. 가스터빈 수요 증가를 감안하면 추가 캐
파 증설 결정이 이르면 연내 이루어질 전망이다. 
 [원전] 10월 NuScale 1개 모듈 제작 계약을 체결했으며, PPA 지연 여부와 
무관하게 완결된 계약으로 실적에 반영될 전망이다. X-energy는 연내 기자
재 및 부품의 후속 계약을 추진 중이다. TerraPower 주기기 3종 중 2종 계
약이 완료되어 제작에 착수했으며, Rolls-Royce SMR·i-SMR 등 파트너십 
확대도 지속되고 있다. 이외에 대미투자 관련 기자재 발주는 미국 부지 확정 
및 한국 의회 승인 절차가 남아있어 내년 발주가 기대된다. 기존 웨스팅하우
스 AP1000 Vogtle 계약 대비 업무 범위 확대(원자로·증기발생기 외 추가 
품목) 가능성이 있어 긍정적으로 평가한다.

### Page 2

두산에너빌리티

2_ Eugene Research Center 
설명회 Q&A 
Q1. 4Q26 SMR 수주 목표 약 1 조원의 실현 가능성 
- 고객사와 GTC 등 주요 계약 조건에 대한 합의를 대부분 완료하고 계약 체결 대기 중 
- 연내 계약 가능성이 있어 수주 목표는 의미 있는 수준

Q2. NuScale 모듈 1 기 제작 계약의 PPA 지연 영향 
- 완결된 제작 계약으로 PPA 체결이 지연되더라도 해당 모듈의 제작 및 진행에는 문제없음

Q3. AP1000 장주기 기자재 선발주 후 인허가 지연 시 회계 처리 및 사업 영향 
- DOE·DOC 및 한국 정부 재원을 활용해 EPC 계약·착공 지연과 무관하게 기자재 제작 가능 
- 프로젝트 중단 시 웨스팅하우스의 다른 프로젝트로 기자재 전용 가능성 검토

Q4. 체코 원전 컨소시엄 변경 시 참여 및 사업 계획 
- 현지 지점 운영과 시공인력 상주로 시공 환경 자료 확보 
- 한수원과 초기 인허가·설계에 참여하고, 주기기 공급·설치 역량을 바탕으로 경쟁력 확보 기대

Q5. 체코 원전 EPC 사업의 수익성 확보 가능성 
- 한수원 가이드에 따라 수익성을 고려한 입찰 진행 예정

Q6. 웨스팅하우스 SPV 및 폴란드 AP1000 장주기 기자재 발주 진행 상황 
- DOE·DOC 프로젝트와 폴란드 AP1000 3 기 기자재 조기 발주 협의 중 
- 연내 수주 여부는 불확실하나 조기 수주 추진

Q7. 3Q26 영업이익률 개선 요인 
- 원자력·가스터빈 중심의 사업 믹스 개선이 수익성에 기여 
- 제작 진행률과 마일스톤에 따라 이익 인식

### Page 3

두산에너빌리티

Eugene Research Center _3 
Q8. 4Q26 복합화력 수주 목표 5,000 억원의 달성 가능성 
- 국내 1 건, 해외 2 건 협의 중이며 국내 프로젝트의 수주 가능성이 높은 상황 
- 대미 투자 물량은 기존 2031 년까지의 예약계약 납기와 비교해 검토 중

Q9. 3Q26 에너빌리티부문 당기순손실 원인 
- 급격한 환율 하락에 따른 약 800 억원의 환산손실이 주요 원인

Q10. 대미 투자 관련 기자재 발주 시점 
- 미국 부지 확정, 웨스팅하우스 사업제안 및 한국 정부 심사·국회 승인 절차 필요 
- 연말~내년 초 발주 여부는 불확실하나 조기 발주 추진

Q11. 대미 투자 프로젝트의 기자재 공급 범위 확대 가능성 
- 원자로·증기발생기 외 추가 품목까지 공급 범위 확대를 위해 웨스팅하우스와 협상 중

Q12. 장주기 기자재 선발주 후 인허가 지연 시 실적 영향 
- 기자재 출하 시 수금 조건이 충족되며 선발주 자금도 사전 확보 
- 보글·VC 서머 사례에서 기자재 대금을 회수해 프로젝트 지연에 따른 실적 영향은 제한적

Q13. 4Q26 신재생·풍력 수주 목표 약 7,000 억원의 달성 가능성 
- 해상풍력 기자재·EPC 계약 및 유지보수 계약 추진 중 
- 주요 계약 협의가 상당 부분 완료돼 일정 지연이 없다면 연내 목표 달성 전망

Q14. 중형 가스터빈 개발 및 해외 진출 계획 
- 대형 가스터빈은 한국·미국 중심으로 추진하며 중동 고객사와도 협의 중 
- 휴스턴 서비스 허브를 기반으로 미국 데이터센터 시장 대응 
- 중형 가스터빈은 시제품 제작 및 시험 진행 후 2028 년 하반기 출하 목표

### Page 4

두산에너빌리티

4_ Eugene Research Center 
Q15. NuScale 모듈 1 기 선발주 배경 
- 대규모 일괄 발주에 대비한 제작 경험 확보 및 납기 리스크 완화를 위해 사전 제작 추진

Q16. X-energy 연내 후속 계약 대상 품목 
- 증기발생기 전열관 및 주단 소재 등 장주기 품목의 사전 발주 추진

Q17. DOE 프로젝트의 장주기 기자재 공급 범위 확대 가능성 
- 웨스팅하우스 공급망 상황에 따라 추가 품목 협상 중 
- 과거 보글·VC 서머보다 넓은 공급 범위 기대

Q18. 웨스팅하우스 지분 투자와 기자재 계약 시점의 연계 가능성 
- 지분 투자는 내년, 기자재 계약은 연내 또는 내년 초 목표로 시점이 일치할지는 불확실

Q19. X-energy 장주기 기자재의 소재·부품 구분 
- 증기발생기 전열관은 부품, 주단 소재는 소재에 해당 
- 전열관은 공급 병목에 대비해 조기 발주하고 주단 소재는 설계 확정 후 제작 준비

Q20. 복합화력 주기기 수주의 기존·신규 고객 비중 
- 기존 고객과 추가 예약계약을 협의 중이며 신규 글로벌 고객과도 예약계약 협상 진행

Q21. 주요 SMR 사업 외 파트너십 확대 가능성 
- Rolls-Royce 사업 추진 및 GE Hitachi BWRX-300 주단 소재 예약계약 체결 
- i-SMR 은 2035 년 운전을 목표로 2028 년 이전 발주가 필요해 관련 협의 진행

Q22. X-energy 전열관·주단 소재 수주 물량 규모 
- 다우케미컬 초도 사업은 80MW 원자로 4 개 모듈로 구성되며, 해당 4 개 모듈분 부품·소재 공급

### Page 5

두산에너빌리티

Eugene Research Center _5 
Q23. 중동 지정학적 불안정에 따른 프로젝트·물류 영향 
- 기존 프로젝트의 운송비 증가분을 발주처·개발사와 협의 중 
- 신규 계약에는 비용 보상 및 공기 지연 관련 조항 반영

Q24. 가스터빈 생산능력 증설 규모 및 시기 
- 현재 생산능력 증설 중이며 2031 년 출하분까지 예약 접수 
- 시장 수요에 따라 추가 증설 검토

Q25. 2030 년 가스터빈 예약 물량의 실제 계약 전환 시점 
- 2030 년 출하 물량은 대부분 내년 실제 계약 여부 결정 전망 
- 2031 년 출하 물량은 2028 년경 계약 여부 결정 예상

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에 동 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

Document 291: Blue Wave_ Democrats sweep the US midterms

Source PDF: all report pdf/Blue Wave_ Democrats sweep the US midterms.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

Blue Wave: Democrats 
sweep the US midterms  
Summary 
Event: The Democrats gained control of the House of 
Representatives and the Senate at the US midterm elections 
that took place this week.  
Our View: With the US government now no longer under 
unified control of the Republicans, gridlock has returned to 
Washington. We believe investors should not overreact to 
these results and should stay invested. 
In equities, investors should continue to diversify their 
equity exposure into more defensive sectors. As we 
transition to mid-cycle for the equity markets, we expect 
stocks to continue to deliver positive returns in the 8-10% 
range per annum, a downshift from the torrid pace of gains in 
the earlier part of the cycle.  
In bonds, income remains attractive given recent spread 
widening. We favour non-hyperscaler investment grade 
corporate bonds at the three to seven-year part of the 
curve. We continue to expect the US dollar to remain range-
bound in the near term, as we expect two more rate hikes 
from the Fed, but as this mini rate hike cycle ends in the 
second quarter of 2027, the USD index (DXY) is likely to 
depreciate towards 98.5 as markets start to anticipate a Fed 
rate cut in the second half of next year.  
Oil is likely to rebound in the short-term on heightened 
geopolitical risk, but as supply chains adapt to the current 
situation and Congress exercises oversight on defense 
spending, WTI prices are likely to drift towards our 12-month 
target of USD 80 per barrel.

Democrats made solid gains across the Senate, House 
of Representatives and State Governors 
Results reported as at 7:45PM Singapore Time

Source: The Wall Street Journal, Standard Chartered

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 5 November 2026

Major implications of Democrat control of 
Congress   
1) Fiscal expansionary policies may slow. Democrats are 
now in position to block any new tax cuts or spending plans 
from the Trump administration. At the same time, any fiscal 
policies proposed by Democrats may also face Trump’s veto. 
Hence the fiscal trajectory of the US will likely remain stuck 
over the next two years. As we get into the second half of 
2027, the US debt ceiling will once again loom, and gridlock 
could potentially bring back government shutdowns. 
However, as they have always done in the past, compromise 
will ultimately prevail, in our opinion.  
2) Trump remains in control of foreign policy. With the 
midterms now out of the way, could Trump decide to re-start 
combat operations with Iran? We believe there is a risk that 
US and Israel – now that both countries’ have concluded 
important elections – will once again be tempted to restart 
combat operations to force an end to the conflict. However, 
the duration of any renewed hostilities may be limited by 
Congress next year as the Democrats will likely withhold 
funding for further military operations. In the near-term, oil 
prices may experience renewed volatility before stabilising at 
a lower level next year. The introduction of new tariffs on 
trading partners may prove harder with Democrat oversight. 
3) AI data centre moratoriums could grow, though model 
development regulatory changes would be harder to 
pass. While the headlines have focused on the House and 
Senate results, one important development has also 
occurred at the gubernatorial level. Notably, Democrats have 
won the governor races in Texas and Georgia, two big data 
centre states. As data centre build out permits are largely 
decided at the local state level, this will have important 
ramifications for build out plans next year. However, any 
Democratic proposals to regulate AI model developments 
would need to be signed off by President Trump, who 
continues to wield veto power on any such proposals.   
Asset class strategy 
Equities: Remain invested and continue to diversify.  
While investors initially overreacted, President Trump’s 
relatively conciliatory response has provided some welcome 
relief, helping markets recover strongly from their pre-market 
lows. Hence, we recommend investors avoid 
overreacting, remain invested and continue to diversify 
their equity exposure across sectors and regions.  
A few themes stand out from the early pre-market reaction to 
the mid-term elections. On the positive side, defensive stocks 
continue to fare well, while themes favoured by Democrats 
are also outperforming. Clean energy is a notable 
beneficiary, with solar stocks among the biggest winners

Policy uncertainty and a change in the innovation cycle 
is consistent with our view of a mid-cycle transition, 
where equity returns moderate but remain positive

today. Affordability-related themes are also rallying, including

Medicaid-related stocks are also moving higher on

On the negative side, semiconductor weakness continues

Our AI capex estimates remain conservative relative to 
consensus, but with a potential reset in expectations around

After downgrading semiconductors last month, we have 
maintained a conservative stance on the industry, with a

While we believe the mid-term election outcome is not a 
complete surprise to markets, particularly given pre-election 
polling and the recent weakness in both the S&P 500 and

investors had become, with the VIX initially surging more than 
10% in pre-market trading, although down from intra-day

As we have highlighted in our recent notes on the transition 
to mid-cycle, this phase is typically characterised by greater 
political headwinds. The mid-term election outcome 
reinforces the trend. The experience of 2015-2017, 
highlighted in the second table, offers a useful historical 
parallel. Despite the knee-jerk reaction, investors should not 
overreact based on one day’s moves. While we had trimmed 
equities to a core allocation last week, we continue to expect 
equities to deliver mid-to-high single-digit returns per annum 
during the mid-cycle, even amid political headwinds, 
supported by steady underlying earnings growth in both 2027 
and 2028. 
So, what should investors do to navigate the next few months 
before the newly elected representatives are sworn in and 
take control of the House and Senate? As we have been 
highlighting in recent notes, equity markets should still deliver 
positive returns, albeit at a more moderate pace. Hence, we 
recommend staying invested but diversification remains key 
to navigating the next phase of the cycle.

98.5. A Democratic sweep potentially removes the legislative 
runway for further tariff escalation and for fiscal expansion, 
the two structural pillars of US exceptionalism that have 
anchored dollar demand in 2026. Reduced fiscal impulse 
compresses the growth and yield differential that has kept the 
US dollar well-bid all year. Additionally, potential Democratic 
restrictions on AI and data centre investment could weigh on 
US equity outperformance, which is likely to act as a 
secondary dollar-negative channel.

Fixed Income: Still attractive all-in yields; prefer 
investment grade corporates at three-to-seven year 
tenors to earn income and manage interest rate risk. The 
US Treasury bond market reaction appears to reflect a 
moderation in expectations of future economic growth after 
the election results. Yields at the intermediate end of the 
curve dropped the most, while the 30-year US yield 
moderated more modestly. We continue to expect two more 
Fed rate hikes between now and H1 2027 as still sticky 
inflation and the robust economy support further tightening.  
In the past few weeks, the US Treasury yield curve has 
broadly shifted downwards, after our opportunistic call to go 
long 10-year Treasuries at the end of September. We are of 
the view the ultra-long end downshift in yields is overdone 
(given the still challenging US fiscal trajectory) and would 
recommend investors who had earlier taken long duration 
exposures at the 20 to 30-year parts of the curve reduce their 
positions now. We continue to believe the 3 to 7-year part of 
the curve offers the most optimal balance of income and 
interest rate risk, particularly via investment corporate bonds. 
Within corporate credit, we expect a potentially slower pace 
of AI data centre buildouts could marginally improve market 
technicals, as hyperscaler and tech sector bond issuance 
may slow (and reduce “crowding out” of other bond 
issuances). Conversely, from a credit perspective, we believe 
this dynamic may also make the data centre focused deals 
less viable, and would be cautious on some of this sector’s 
lower quality names. Meanwhile, less AI capex dependent 
issuers continue to offer solid credit profiles amidst the still 
strong economy. High yield corporate spreads have widened 
in recent weeks but dispersion within the complex is wide, 
with BBs and Bs credit spreads staying relatively resilient, 
while CCCs have widened to distressed levels. For income-
focused investors, we would recommend staying within the 
crossover range of between BBB-BB.  
Commodities: As noted in the opening section, we believe 
there is a higher near-term risk of a restart of combat 
operations in Iran given the conclusion of the midterm 
elections. Given that, we believe there is a risk that oil 
prices could see a short-term spike, despite recent 
diplomatic overtures from Iran. However, for 2027 we 
continue to expect oil to stabilise at lower levels, as we 
think the Mideast situation should de-escalate given 
Democratic oversight of the defense budget, and the likely re-
configuration of oil supply chains. On the margin, we are also 
closely monitoring regulatory developments in the US shale

Document 317: Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation

Source PDF: all report pdf/Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

Downgrade of global 
equities to a Core allocation  
Summary 
Event: We are downgrading global equities from Overweight 
to a Neutral/Core allocation. We continue to maintain a 
Neutral allocation to fixed income, Overweight on Gold and 
Underweight on Cash. There are more details in our Global 
Market Outlook publication on 30 October 2026. 
What does the equity downgrade mean?  
This downgrade implies a slightly lower allocation to global 
equities within a diversified portfolio. For our CIO Foundation 
Balanced portfolio, this would imply an equity allocation of 
52.5%, down from 53.2%. We continue to expect positive 
returns for global equities in the coming 6-12 months, 
though equity investors may need to recalibrate expectations 
lower, compared with the 15-20% annualised gains 
experienced in the last few years. Our capital market 
assumptions still suggest a mid-to-high single digit annual 
returns over the next few years, consistent with a transition 
to a mid-cycle bull market. Consensus estimates point to 
16% earnings growth for global equities in 2027, which 
should overcome various headwinds to deliver positive 
returns for equities over the next 12 months. Investors should 
thus, continue to maintain exposure to equities within a 
diversified portfolio. 
In addition, we see a changing playbook to prepare for 
higher volatility, more active management and greater 
diversification, as discussed below. 
What does it mean for regional equities 
positioning? 
Within a global equities portfolio, we remain Overweight US 
and Asia ex-Japan (AxJ) equities, which are expected to 
deliver the highest earnings growth in 2027 amongst the 
major regions. This growth is led by the technology sector, 
which carries the largest weight in both regions. Within the 
diversified equities portfolio, we maintain Core allocations to

Euro area equities, which offer lower valuations, and Japan 
equities, which is backed by a reflating economy. We are 
Underweight UK equities, where the sector composition 
offers lower growth prospects in 2027. Within the AxJ region, 
we are Overweight Taiwan equities, with attractive structural 
growth driven by its semiconductor industry. We are also 
Overweight India equities, which offers diversified non-tech 
growth drivers. Taiwan and India equities both generate 
attractive returns on equity. We maintain Core allocations to 
China equities, with low valuations, and Korea equities, with 
explosive earnings growth in its semiconductors industry, but 
volatile price movements. We are Underweight ASEAN, 
which offers relatively lower earnings growth. 
What are the implications for equity sectors? 
We are Overweight these sectors globally: US 
information technology, US healthcare, US financials, US 
materials, Europe ex-UK financials, and Japan 
financials. 
A framework we use is to break the market into four key 
sector building blocks. Tech is the largest block, 
encompassing the Information Technology, Communication 
Services and Consumer Discretionary sectors. Put 
differently, the segment spans five major industries: 
semiconductors, hardware, internet, software, and IT 
services. Within the US market, we expect this segment to 
deliver c.30% earnings growth in 2027, extending the strong 
momentum seen in 2026. 
Financials and Real Estate are the next building block, 
comprising these two sectors. We expect this segment to 
continue delivering low-teens earnings growth in 2027, 
supported by resilient economic activity and healthy credit 
trends. 
Resources include companies across four sectors: 
Industrials, Materials, Energy, and Utilities. We expect the 
segment to generate 10-15% earnings growth in 2027, 
although Energy is likely to remain the largest drag on overall 
growth due to tougher comparisons and softer energy price 
assumptions for 2027. 
Healthcare & Others consists primarily of Healthcare and 
Consumer Staples. We expect mid-teen earnings growth for 
the segment in 2027, with a rebound in healthcare earnings 
representing a potential upside surprise. 
Our Overweight sector exposures are drawn from each of the 
four key blocks above. Unlike the last few years, with 
concentrated gains from Tech providing market leadership, 
we see our downgrade in global equities also reflecting a 
broadening in market leadership towards each of the other 
sector blocks apart from Tech.

As structural themes such as AI transition from the rapid 
buildout phase towards monetisation, diversified exposure 
across sectors and regions should become increasingly 
important. 
What are the implications for equity 
opportunistic ideas? 
We have opportunistic ideas on Global High-Dividend 
equities, US healthcare, Euro area banks, MSCI 
Switzerland and Japan banks. The selection represents a 
more defensive stance currently, with an average beta of 
below 1 relative to global equities (MSCI AC World), but 
attractive drivers nonetheless, for each of the ideas.  
Global High-Dividend offers higher-income equity allocation 
with historically lower volatility than global equities. US 
healthcare is supported by wider adoption of obesity 
treatments, advances in oncology and innovative medical 
devices, that support earnings growth. Euro area banks 
continue to see steady earnings growth adding to their 
excess capital, which is being returned to shareholders via 
attractive dividend and share buyback yields. Japan banks 
continue to enjoy net margin expansion from BoJ policy 
normalisation, as well as corporate governance 
improvements. MSCI Switzerland is a newly initiated idea for 
investors seeking defensive exposure. Healthcare and 
consumer staples accounts for more than half the index 
weight, providing exposure to relatively resilient demand that 
is more insulated from economic cycles and AI trends. It also 
has a sizeable financial sector supported by steady wealth 
management growth and well-capitalised insurers with 
disciplined underwriting and resilient premium income. 
We see the case for more active management in a mid-
cycle transition, as higher market dispersion should create 
greater opportunities for active sector and stock selection, 
particularly as leadership within equities evolves. 
What are the implications of higher equity 
market volatility?  
A more uncertain political backdrop and potentially maturing 
growth rates in certain market segments may contribute to 
more frequent bouts of market volatility. Higher volatility 
presents an opportunity to use structured strategies to gain 
exposure to equities. Such strategies may be used to 
generate income or to gain exposure at a discounted entry 
price. Investors can also consider strategies that are 
structured to return principal or partial principal at maturity. 
What alternative strategies are suited to a 
regime of moderating equity market returns?  
Investors can consider alternative strategies such as hedge 
fund strategies or private markets, for those with the capacity

to accept some illiquidity.  
Equity long/short hedge fund strategies would retain growth 
participation with less market exposure. Fund managers buy 
companies they expect to outperform and short those they 
expect to underperform, seeking to earn the difference 
between the two, and benefit even as total equity market 
returns are moderating. 
Within private markets, private equity provides a means to 
higher returns with the effects of an illiquidity premium. 
Meanwhile, infrastructure offers differentiated exposure to 
structural growth opportunities, where contracted long-term 
revenues reduce dependence on equity-market sentiment. 
What are the implications on fixed income 
and FX markets? 
Bonds: This year’s surge in bond yields means bonds now 
offer a hedge against any equity market weakness. We 
believe the 3-7-year part of the US bond yield curve offers 
the best hedge against any equity sell-off caused by AI-
related growth worries. This part of the bond curve has priced 
in most Fed rate hike expectations, which could reverse if 
growth concerns return. Meanwhile, we favour US Treasury 
Inflation-Protected Securities (TIPS) as a hedge against any 
fuel price-led inflation scare.  
Credit: For corporate bonds, adopting a defensive stance in 
a volatile environment typically involves: i) increasing credit 
quality, favouring higher-rated issuers; and ii) moving up the 
capital structure, by reducing exposure to subordinated debt 
and favouring secured debt. From a sector perspective, 
large, diversified banks, utilities, and quasi-sovereigns tend 
to be more defensive, particularly relative to cyclical sectors, 
such as metals and mining, exploration, and technology.         
FX: We favour bearish AUD/JPY as a tactical hedge against 
any equity market correction. Review of 21 past S&P500 
correction sessions (2010-2025) shows AUD weakened 
against USD in 86% of cases, while JPY strengthened 
against USD in 67%, supporting our bearish AUD/JPY 
outlook during any equity market stress.

Document 334: France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08)

Source PDF: all report pdf/France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 1
Status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 8 October 2026

EcoFlash

France: double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and 
stable non-performing loans ratio

Business creations rose by 12% y/y over the first eight months of 20261. This is significantly higher than the 5.3% y/y 
growth recorded in 2025. This increase was driven by sectors linked to information and communication technologies.

Meanwhile, business bankruptcies rose by 5.3% y/y between January and July, according to data from the Banque de 
France (compared with the 3.6% y/y increase for the whole of 2025). The rise was more pronounced in the 1st quarter (+8% y/y) 
before slowing down. This slowdown could persist, as, according to Altares, payment delays have fallen to an average of 12.3 
days in the second quarter of 2026, compared with 14.1 days a year earlier (and 13.1 days for the European average).

Rising bankruptcies are more heavily affecting the services sector, which is also experiencing slightly stronger growth 
in business creations. This correlation is particularly noticeable in the retail sector, as business creations have risen by 13% 
since the start of 2026, while bankruptcies are up by 3%. In construction and manufacturing, business bankruptcies have been 
falling since the second quarter of 2026.

The average ratio of non-performing corporate loans has remained stable at a moderately high level for the past year 
(4.0% of total loans and advances in Q2 2026). In construction, where activity is more cyclical than in other sectors, the ratio of 
non-performing loans has reached its highest level since 2019, standing at 9.5%.

The proportion of loans that have deteriorated but are not yet non-performing (“Stage 2” according to IFRS 9 terminology) 
is stable, standing at 15.4% q/q, and remains below its all-time high (16.1%). This indicator, which helps to anticipate trends in 
non-performing loans, is relatively encouraging.

Despite a significant increase since 2021, the economic weight of bankruptcies remains, on average, below its peak in 
Q1 2025 (0.53 compared with 0.61, respectively). This indicator generally allows bankruptcies to be weighted based on the size 
of the businesses. However, the economic weight of bankrupted small businesses is at a historically high level (2.11).

Take a look at our EcoCharts on trends in business creations and bankruptcies in France and the 
associated risks. It will be updated on a quarterly basis.

Thomas Humblot 
Economiste, team: Banking economics 
thomas.humblot@bnpparibas.com

Stéphane Colliac 
Head of Advanced economies, France 
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

1 For more details, see Business creations and bankruptcies in France: double-digit growth in business creations, lower growth in bankruptcies, 
and stable non-performing loans, 8 October 2026.

分化 台系ODM 同時承接AMD Helios與Helion者具備較高訂單調整彈性，有望降低單一GPU供應商 的營運風險；高度集中輝達平台者承壓

相對受壓 NeoCloud CoreWeave、Nebius、Nscale兼具輝達股東、供應商與擔保人三重關係；Nvidia若 因管理層變動調整投資、採購或融資支持，或衝擊營收成長、資金取得及估值
政策變數 出口管制 共和黨失去國會多數，新國會可能推動更嚴格對中晶片管制；H200對中出貨政策面 臨重審
外資觀點彙整
機構 評等／動作 核心論點
Baird 降至中立；TP 500→200美元 CEO更替機率升高且外部人選勝算較高；聯邦調查與財務影響量化前，股價最多區間整理
Morgan Stanley 移出首選；維持加碼、TP 300美元不變 黃仁勳的領導對公司策略方向至關重要，短期不確定性將延續
Bernstein 降至中立；TP 400→200美元 「黃仁勳就是輝達」；董事會調查與晶片轉運執法審查不論其去留都將持續
BofA 維持買進；TP 350 不變，移出首選 同步將輝達、美光與科林研發三家半導體大廠移出首選清單，對整體半導體產業短線漲幅與估值波動的調整

Source PDF: all report pdf/SpaceX周四拟发债60亿美元，完成全部320亿美元债券融资，用于购买英伟达芯片.pdf

### Page 1

据媒体报道，本次发售包括约 60 亿美元债券，规模已从此前的 40 亿美元上调，于周四向投
资者开放认购，其余约 260 亿美元票据此前已完成配售。资⾦将通过名为 Saturn Funding
Trust 的特殊⽬的载体购买英伟达 AI 芯⽚， SpaceX 初期以五年期租约使⽤这些芯⽚。
摘要：
SpaceX 正寻求在周四完成⾄少 320 亿美元的债务发售，为英伟达 AI 芯⽚采购提供资⾦。
据彭博援引知情⼈⼠透露，本次发售包括约 60 亿美元债券，规模已从此前的 40 亿美元上调，于
周四向投资者开放认购；其余约 260 亿美元票据此前已完成配售。全部 320 亿美元债务定于周四
定价，债务由三部分组成。⾼盛与 Apollo 主导此次交易。
这笔融资的特殊之处在于结构。资⾦将通过名为 Saturn Funding Trust 的特殊⽬的载体购买英
伟达 AI 芯⽚，该载体由 Apollo ⽀持的⼀家实体管理， SpaceX 初期以约五年期租约使⽤这些芯
⽚。
债务偿付与租⾦挂钩，⽽融资安排使借款不进⼊ SpaceX 资产负债表，公司预计借此维持投资级

信⽤评级。在 AI 竞赛中，⾼评级往往被视为关键竞争优势，它意味着能更快、更多地借钱。
SpaceX 在 10 ⽉表⽰，⻉莱德、⾼盛资产管理以及 KKR 集团旗下的账⼾均是最新这笔交易的基⽯
投资者。本次发⾏依据美国证券交易委员会（ SEC ）的相关规定进⾏，可向符合条件的境内外
机构⼴泛营销，但不得⾯向部分零售投资者。
截⾄发稿， SpaceX 股价周四盘中下跌 4.5% ，英伟达股价下跌 2.3% 。
三层结构，⼀笔出表融资
这笔债务被拆分为三部分，具体条款未对外披露。

写评论
60 亿美元债券⾯向投资者公开发售，规模较早前计划增加 20 亿美元，显⽰认购需求强劲；剩余
约 260 亿美元票据在此前已直接配售给资⾦管理机构。
对这⼀结构⽽⾔，真正的关键在于会计处理：芯⽚由特殊⽬的载体持有， SpaceX 以承租⼈⾝份
出现，债务不计⼊公司资产负债表。
对⼀家正在⼤举投⼊ AI 基础设施的公司来说，这⼀点⾄关重要。科技⾏业正把数以万亿美元计的
资⾦投向数据中⼼与 AI 基础设施，并⼤量举债⽀持。评级⼀旦被下调，融资成本和融资速度都会
⽴刻受到影响。
维持⾼评级，是竞争优势
SpaceX ⾸席财务官 Bret Johnsen 在上⽉发布业绩后的投资者电话会上，强调了公司 “ 强劲的投资
级资产负债表 ” 。
这⼀表述出现在公司完成⼤规模股权与债券融资之后： SpaceX 在 6 ⽉的⾸次公开募股中售出 862

亿美元股票，数⽇后⼜定价发⾏ 250 亿美元⾼评级公司债券。
本次芯⽚融资延续了同⼀思路 —— 在不推⾼资产负债表杠杆的前提下，继续扩⼤算⼒投⼊。将
债务置于表外，使公司能够在不牺牲评级的情况下加杠杆，这对处在资本开⽀竞赛中的⼤型科
技企业尤为重要。
分销更⼴，买家更多
SEC 规则下的发⾏⽅式，让这笔债券可以触达更⼴的机构群体。
Manulife Investment Management 固收⾼级投资组合经理 Jeff Given 表⽰，这带来了⼴泛分销与
灵活性，意味着债券可能 “ 落⼊更多投资者⼿中 ” 。对于⼀笔规模超过 300 亿美元的发⾏⽽⾔，投
资者基础的宽度直接关系到定价与⼆级市场表现。
从 6 ⽉的 IPO 、随后的⾼评级债券，到如今的芯⽚租赁融资， SpaceX 在数⽉内搭建起⼀套多层次
的资本结构。周四的定价结果，将检验市场对 AI 相关债务的胃⼝还能承接多⼤的供给。

The scenario, forecasts and nowcasts of the Economic Research 
– 
16 
Novem
ber 2026

### Page 2

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2.2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%). 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.5% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect two further 25bp increases in December 2026 and January 2027, bringing the Fed Funds 
target range at 4.25% - 4.50%.

CHINA 
Economic growth rebounded to 4.5% y/y in Q3 2026 (1.2% q/q) after 4.3% y/y in Q2 and 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a 
whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth remains characterised by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports 
and the fragility of sectors that rely on domestic demand continues to widen. Retail sales barely increased in the first ten months of 2026, the 
crisis in the property sector continues and household confidence remains low. Yet new fiscal policy measures could help support investment 
and private consumption in the very short term. Deflationary pressures are persisting, given weakness in domestic demand. Energy prices and 
electronic goods prices are increasing, producer price inflation is accelerating (5.7% y/y in October), but consumer price inflation remains low 
(0.7% y/y).

EUROZONE 
The economy has shown resilience in the face of the energy shock: GDP grew by 0.4% q/q in Q3, and the 1.0% growth carry-over for 2026 
supports our forecast of 1.1% for the year as a whole (compared to 1.3% in 2025) with the risk tilted to the upside. Growth is expected to 
strengthen to 1.6% in 2027, bolstered by increased spending on defence and infrastructure, as well as the global investment cycle in AI. Annual 
average inflation is thus expected to reach 3.1% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), and 3.2% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, 
we anticipate another 25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause. That 
said, stronger than expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

### Page 3

FRANCE 
GDP contracted by 0.1% q/q in Q3 2026 (following stagnation in Q2), weighed down by a decline in aeronautics production (delay in the 
delivery of inputs) but supported by service sector output and purchases of electric vehicles. Overall, growth is expected to slow to 0.3% in 
2026 (after 0.9% in 2025) before rebounding to 0.8% in 2027. In parallel, inflation should reach 2.5% in 2026 (compared with 0.9% in 2025), 
before edging down slightly in 2027 (2.4%).

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would accelerate to 1.6% in 2026 (1.2% in 2025). The economy has so far weathered both the energy price shock and domestic 
political volatility, boosted by domestic demand, notably investment. After a series of strong prints from Q1 to Q3, real GDP growth is expected 
to decelerate in the last quarter of the year, as higher energy prices should feed inflation. It should then slow to 1.3% in 2027 on the back of 
higher inflation and more restrictive monetary conditions. Inflation should climb to 3.9% in Q4 2026, peaking in Q1 2027 (at 4.5%) before 
gradually slowing. Still, it is set to remain stubbornly above target, leading to mounting second-round effects risks. In response, the BoE has 
finally raised its Bank Rate to 4.00% early November. If a second-rate hike looks likely in February, this is not, for now, our call and our baseline 
scenario projects that diminishing inflation will allow monetary easing to resume in the second half of 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank 
Rate down to 3.50% by year-end.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 0.9% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.0% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of +2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and  
30-year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the dollar. EUR/USD would target 1.14 by end-2026, before the 
dollar's structural downtrend resumes toward 1.20 by end-2027, although political and fiscal risk may weigh on the euro next year. The USD/JPY 
appreciation would continue but at a more moderate pace, with the BoJ still behind the curve despite its hikes. By contrast, GBP/USD would 
see relative support underpinned by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government, and resilient growth.

Document 428: What lessons can we learn to keep European industry competitive from the measures taken by Germany, as the cost of electricity continues to turn the screw_ (2026-10-22)

WHAT LESSONS CAN WE LEARN TO KEEP EUROPEAN INDUSTRY COMPETITIVE FROM
THE MEASURES TAKEN BY GERMANY, AS THE COST OF ELECTRICITY CONTINUES TO
TURN THE SCREW?
Marianne Mueller and Pascal Devaux
issue 19
21 October 2026
The 2022 energy crisis clearly highlighted the issue of electricity prices in Europe — and, more broadly, the issue of energy prices. As
a result, European manufacturers’ competitiveness has been severely undermined when compared with the United States and China.

By reigniting tensions over electricity prices, the conflict that began in the Middle East in late February 2026 further underscores the
continent’s vulnerability to fossil fuel imports, despite the expansion of renewable energy. Faced with an environment of potential sharp
and sudden price rises, European governments do not have the same room for manoeuvre.
While the additional costs borne by European manufacturers are mainly linked to the share of gas in national electricity mixes, we will

first illustrate that, in Germany, grid operation and maintenance costs, as well as taxation, are also contributing significantly to these
costs.
We will then explore the solutions proposed by the German government under the new European measures regulating state aid to
electricity-intensive industries.
Finally, our estimates will show that, while these measures make Germany more competitive against some European countries, they
do not place it on an equal competitive footing with the United States or China. Nevertheless, Germany has made significant progress

in decarbonising and making its electricity mix more flexible from a European perspective. This should help to ensure that electricity costs
are reduced in the medium term.

### Page 2

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoInsight 19 / 21 October 2026

worldWHAT LESSONS CAN WE LEARN TO KEEP EUROPEAN INDUSTRY COMPETITIVE FROM THE MEA- SURES TAKEN BY GERMANY, AS THE COST OF ELECTRICITY CONTINUES TO TURN THE SCREW?
GAS: A KEY FACTOR IN THE ADDITIONAL ELECTRICITY COSTS
FACED BY EUROPEAN INDUSTRY
European manufacturers are still paying significantly higher elec -
tricity costs than their American and Chinese competitors. In 2019,

there was a 30% difference between the prices charged to European
electricity-intensive industries (corresponding to the lower end of the
industrial price range) and the average US industrial price. In 2025,
it stood at nearly 100% according to the IEA (2026) 1, after peaking at
an average of 148% between 2022 and 2023. There is currently a 50%
difference with their Chinese competitors2.
The disruption to Russian gas supplies since 2022 is behind the rising

gas prices, particularly as the EU has had to turn to liquefied natural
gas3 (LNG), which is more expensive than gas transported via pipe -
line and, above all, exposes Europe to global gas market conditions.
The growth of LNG has, in fact, intensified competition between the
major consumption regions, primarily Europe and Asia. European and
Asian prices now tend to move more in tandem (Chart 1). However, in
China, the prevalence of coal — a widely available local resource —

and a more regulated electricity price help to keep industrial prices
lower. At the same time, the United States enjoys plentiful domestic
gas, which is produced at a low cost, driven by the sharp rise in shale
oil and gas production over the past decade. By contrast, since 2022,
Europe has been more exposed to the cost of imported gas (90% of its
total consumption, according to Eurostat), while the United States and
China have enjoyed plentiful domestic resources.

Fluctuations and the level of gas prices are passed on to Eu -
ropean electricity prices via the ‘merit order’ marginal pricing
mechanism, with the wholesale price determined by the margi -
nal cost of the last (and most expensive) electricity source called
upon to meet demand.
As a result, gas plays a decisive role, with gas-fired power sta -
tions setting the price in around 40% of cases in Europe4, with si-
gnificant variations depending on national electricity mixes. It is

close to this average in Germany, below it in Spain (26%) and
France (17%), and above it in Italy (73%) 5 in 2025 6 7. Therefore,
electricity prices are still sensitive to fluctuations in gas prices,
even when electricity is not generated from gas (Chart 2). This
pass-through is particularly pronounced when demand rises
and requires the use of more expensive sources, or when supply
constraints arise (for example, during extreme heatwaves that
reduce wind-power generation or disrupt the cooling of nuclear

power plants).
1 Prices – Electricity 2026 – Analysis – IEA
2 The difference may be even larger for other manufacturers who do not receive the same support as electricity-intensive sectors.
3 Imports of Russian gas (via pipeline and LNG) accounted for around 45% of total European imports in 2021, compared with 14% in Q2 2026 (Bruegel, natural gas imports).
4 ACER, 2026, Key developments in EU electricity and gas markets, 2026 Monitoring report.
5 In Italy, in response to sharply rising gas prices in 2026, the government is currently negotiating a potential option of capping the prices of gas supplied to power stations in

order to limit the rise in the wholesale price of electricity. However, this can only be a temporary general mechanism, with the potentially major cost being borne by public funds
and/or households.
6 Eurelectric, 2026, Power Barometer.
7 EMBER, 2026, Latest energy shock reminds Europe of its risky reliance on gas.
8 Energy shock: 2026 vs. 2022 dashboard – 2nd October 2026 – Economic Studies – BNP Paribas
However, for the time being, the 2026 energy crisis is having a far

smaller effect on electricity prices than in 2022. On the one hand, the
2026 peak reached by the European TTF benchmark is still well below
that recorded in 20228.
In addition, there has been significant progress in decarbonising Eu -
ropean electricity mixes since 2022, which has helped to mitigate, on
average, the pass-through of gas prices to electricity prices.

SOME EUROPEAN COUNTRIES HAVE MANAGED TO STAY
COMPETITIVE, BUT GERMANY IS STILL THE EXCEPTION
Electricity prices partly vary due to countries’ electricity mixes
(Chart 3). Electricity-intensive manufacturers in countries where the
share of fossil energy is high face higher costs (Poland, Italy and Ire -
land) (Chart 4). Conversely, countries with a less carbon-intensive mix

(particularly France and Spain in recent times) are less directly ex -
posed to these fluctuations, with costs closer to the United States and
China (Chart 5).
The situation in Germany is paradoxical, as, despite progressively
greening its electricity mix and wholesale electricity prices (averaging
90 EUR/MWh over the 2023–2025 period) and average electricity costs
(across all industrial sectors) in line with the European average, the most
energy-intensive industries face higher costs than the European average

(and even more so compared with international competitors). This is
mainly due to grid costs and a tax burden above EU standards (Chart 6).
9 Eurostat data are used to compare prices with the rest of the EU. However, given that electricity prices are not uniform for businesses at a European level, there may be differences
between Eurostat and national sources. Jäger P., 2025, “Cautionary tale or model for Europe? How member states should react to Germany’s new electricity subsidy”, Hertie School,

Jacques Delors Centre.
10 P. Devaux, 2026, “European Union: low carbon transition and energy sovereignty, a path fraught with obstacles”
In the first half of 2026, for the most electricity-intensive industries,
the difference compared with the European average was 9%, based on
the prices excluding taxes and levies provided by Eurostat. This gap is
mainly due to the cost of accessing the grid. If all taxes and levies are
included, the difference rises to 21% (Chart 7)9.

EUROPE’S APPROACH OF ADOPTING BOTH STRUCTURAL
PROGRAMMES AND TARGETED SUPPORT MEASURES
European programmes to support the low-carbon transition pursue
objectives relating to climate action (reducing greenhouse gas emis -
sions), sovereignty10 (increasing energy self-sufficiency) and competi -
tiveness (reducing the volatility and costs associated with fossil fuels).
The RePowerEU programme, which was launched in 2022 in response
to the energy crisis, primarily aims to reduce imports of Russian gas

and, more generally, to accelerate the low-carbon transition. Although
Europe has reduced its dependence on imports of Russian fossil fuels,
there has still not been enough progress around the energy transition
and cross-border connectivity. This is due to governance constraints

and struggles in implementing the programme in practice within each
Member State11.
State aid is still the main vehicle for this. In response to the 2021–
2022 energy crisis, the European Union temporarily relaxed the rules
on this type of aid in order to support businesses facing rising energy
prices. Any intervention of this kind, provided that it is selective and

could affect trade within the single market, must be authorised in ad -
vance by the European Commission in principle. Since June 2025, the
Clean Industrial Deal State Aid Framework (CISAF) has provided a more
sustainable framework for these kinds of interventions. In particular,
this framework, which runs until the end of 2030, authorises Member
States to grant temporary aid to electricity-intensive businesses to off-
set the cost of electricity. The aid must be targeted and come with

decarbonisation commitments12. Against the backdrop of the crisis in
the Middle East, the temporary METSAF scheme, adopted in April 2026,
helped (in relation to measures falling under the CISAF) to increase the
proportion of additional costs offset by governments from 50% to 70%,
thereby offering additional support to the most at-risk sectors.
GERMANY IS STILL SUFFERING FROM INSUFFICIENT IN-
VESTMENT IN ITS ELECTRICITY NETWORKS, WHICH THE
AID SCHEMES ARE ATTEMPTING TO OFFSET

The issue of electricity costs is a particularly hot issue in Germany,
given the importance of industry to the economy (around 20% of va -
lue added, one of the highest levels in Europe) and its exposure to
international competition. Furthermore, the delay in modernising
electricity infrastructure (only 22.6% of the high-voltage lines planned
for 2025 had actually been completed by that date) is causing bottle -
necks between the production areas in the north and the consump -

11 European Court of Auditors, 2026, “Implementation of the REPowerEU plan must be stepped up”.
12 It applies to the most electricity-intensive activities, which are seeing their competitiveness threatened by international competition, and follows the 4x50 principle: 50% of
electricity consumption is eligible for a subsidy; Of the eligible proportion, the subsidy may not exceed 50% of the wholesale price;
The final wholesale price paid by the company must not be less than €50/MWh;

50% of the aid received must be reinvested in decarbonisation (renewable energy generation, storage and improvements in energy efficiency); Around 90 sectors are covered by this
reform, accounting for approximately 2,000 companies, including in the chemical, steel and paper industries. The scheme, which is strictly regulated, will run for just three years
(from 1st January 2026 to the end of 2028) at an estimated total cost of between EUR 3 and 5 bn, financed by the special Climate and Transformation Fund.

13 Bundesnetzagentur Monitoring Report (2015) and Bundesnetzagentur Monitoring Report (2025). These costs mainly cover the costs of physical infrastructure; operational,
regulatory and compliance costs; and costs associated with using the transmission and distribution network.
14 The Industriestrompreis (industrial electricity price) is the public subsidy mechanism aiming to reduce the price of electricity for some industries.
15 The indirect carbon cost compensation mechanism is a government support measure for electricity-intensive industries, which aims to offset the cost of carbon-emission

allowances for energy producers.
tion centres in the south. As a result, the costs of accessing services
have increased fivefold over 10 years (rising from EUR 1 bn in 2014
to EUR 5.2 bn in 2024 13). At the same time, as we have already seen,
taxation and grid charges are hitting industry hard, with levies on large
consumers far exceeding the European average despite some support
mechanisms being put in place for the most electricity-intensive com-

panies (such as the Bandlastprivileg measure to reduce grid tariffs).
As a result, German industrial production has suffered in particular
due to rising energy prices in recent years, including electricity, with
electricity-intensive industries being especially affected (Chart 8).
In order to cope with these higher electricity costs than the European
average, Germany has been using European support schemes to reduce
the cost of electricity for businesses, particularly since the Merz go -

vernment took office in May 2025. The main scheme — the Industries-
trompreis14, made possible by the CISAF — has been approved by the
European Commission for the 2026–2028 period. While it is less bene-
ficial than the indirect carbon cost (ICC) offsetting included in electri -
city prices15, it has a broader scope, meaning that businesses ineligible
for the ICC can benefit from it. Other measures complement this targe-
ted support by addressing other components of the electricity bill. As a

result, the electricity tax has been set at the European minimum for
manufacturing sectors, while the gas-storage tax has been abolished.
The government is also subsidising grid fees, which should halve them.
Overall, according to our estimates based on 2024 prices, the final
price of electricity for these industries could fall by around a quarter
(to 116 EUR/MWh for industries with annual consumption of between
70 and 150 GWh, compared with a European average of 123 EUR/MWh)

(Chart 9). Germany’s advantage over Central and Eastern European
countries, where electricity costs are generally already higher, looks
set to increase.

However, even when taking all of the subsidies into account, the price
of electricity in Germany would remain higher than the levels seen in
France, Spain or Portugal (and even more so in the United States and
China).
FISCAL MEASURES: LIMITED ROOM FOR MANOEUVRE
There are a number of limitations to these European schemes. For
the country implementing them, lower electricity costs mean that

its industry becomes more competitive against industries from other
EU countries, but this does very little to address the disadvantage that
it faces compared with the United States and China. Therefore, this
measure will unlikely be enough to counteract the loss of market share
seen vis-à-vis the United States and China. In addition, the support
schemes are restrictive (conditional on investment in the low-carbon
transition) and are transitional. Finally, their cost is partly passed on

to the public purse or to consumers who do not receive this support
(in Germany, the cost of the new energy price support measures is
estimated at EUR 14.3 bn in 2026, or 0.3% of GDP16).
Some European manufacturers have proposed abolishing or reducing
the carbon tax as a solution to getting electricity prices down quickly.
In Germany, this tax accounted for 14% of the electricity price in the
first half of 2025, which is higher than the EU average (11%). However,

this proposal is already covered by the indirect carbon cost offsetting
mechanism for electricity-intensive industries exposed to carbon al -
lowance prices. In addition, while adjustments are being made — no -
tably to slow the rate at which allowances are reduced — it seems
highly unlikely that the scheme will be significantly challenged. It is
one of the cornerstones of Europe’s policy to combat climate change,
which has been effective thus far, as shown by the falling greenhouse

gas emissions from industry. More broadly, the gradual introduction of
a carbon border adjustment mechanism (CBAM) should mitigate com-
petition distortions caused by environmental practices17.
IN GERMANY AND THE REST OF EUROPE, HEAVY INVEST -
MENT IS DRIVING UP ELECTRICITY BILLS IN THE SHORT
TERM
In response to the challenge of the low-carbon transition and indus -
trial competitiveness, European governments must strike a balance
between decarbonising the electricity mix and ensuring a supply of

energy that is flexible enough (particularly from controllable sources)
to guarantee stable electricity systems. The integration of an increa -
sing proportion of intermittent renewable energy sources requires
massive investment in transmission and storage infrastructure (statio-
nary batteries). This expenditure, which places a long-term burden on
grid costs, reduces the scope for support schemes (particularly public
ones) to address competitiveness gaps. In Germany, the cost of the

transition is estimated at between EUR 122 bn, according to the go -
vernment (2.8% of GDP), and EUR 200 bn, according to the DIHK18 (4.6%
of GDP), by 2030–2035. If we take the ECB’s assumption19 that govern-
ments will cover around 17% of these investments, the annual public
16 The Industriestrompreis is estimated at around EUR 1.3 bn per year, the subsidy for network charges is projected to amount to EUR 6.5 bn in 2026, the abolition of the gas sur -

charge around EUR 3.4 bn, and the reduction in the electricity tax nearly EUR 3 bn per year. Furthermore, the ICC had cost EUR 2.3 bn in 2024 for costs incurred in 2023.

expenditure burden of the transition in Germany would be between
0.5 and 0.8 percentage points of GDP by 2030. However, this budgetary
pressure comes at the same time as three other budgetary increases:
rising social expenditure linked to an ageing population; the necessary
increase to defence budgets to meet NATO’s target of 3.5% of GDP by
2029; and the rising cost of debt-servicing20.
Germany has already made significant progress in maximising its use

of renewable energy, putting it at the head of the pack in Europe. The
country generates 20% of Europe’s renewable electricity (solar and
wind) and accounts for 30% of installed storage capacity (BESS) 21, far
ahead of other European countries (ranking third globally). However,
despite this momentum, Germany’s dependence on gas has not yet
decreased. As a matter of fact, it is only partially offsetting the planned
closure of coal-fired power stations and the phasing out of nuclear

reactors. Furthermore, the intermittent nature of solar and wind power
could prolong this dependence in the short and medium term. In order
to ensure that the German electricity system is flexible, new invest -
ments in gas-fired power plants, supported by public subsidies, are
planned22.
